## Flap control
*PDF page 42 · printed page 34*

### Français

**COMMANDE DES VOLETS DE COURBURE**
**schéma de la commande** Pl. 20

La tige du piston d'un vérin hydraulique à double effet est reliée par bielles et tubes de torsion aux articulations de volets de courbure.

Le vérin est commandé par un distributeur à 3 positions (descente - arrêt - relevage) situé à gauche du poste de pilotage.

Pression d'utilisation maximum : 100 kg/cm2.

Les volets ne devront être utilisés que pour une vitesse inférieure à 270 Km/h.

Ramener le levier à la position "ARRET" après chaque ouverture ou fermeture des volets.

### English

**FLAP CONTROL**
**diagram of the control** Pl. 20

The piston rod of a double-acting hydraulic actuator (jack) is connected by connecting rods and torque tubes to the hinges of the flaps.

The actuator is controlled by a 3-position selector valve (down - stop - up) located on the left of the cockpit.

Maximum working pressure: 100 kg/cm2.

The flaps must be used only at speeds below 270 km/h.

Return the lever to the "ARRET" (STOP) position after each opening or closing of the flaps.

*Notes: Printed page 34. Headings underlined. 'ARRET' in the last paragraph is faint but readable. Archive stamp at upper right. Lower two-thirds of page blank.

## Equipment and armament; A - VIET starter
*PDF page 43 · printed page 35*

### Français

**MATERIEL D'EQUIPEMENT ET D'ARMEMENT -**

Les principaux appareils formant l'équipement de l'avion sont:

Le démarreur VIET, le système de freins OLAER, l'extincteur d'incendie LEVY, l'inhalateur MUNERELLE, le poste de T.S.F. O.T.C.537, les instruments de bord et de navigation, l'armement.

A - **Démarreur VIET** ( voir notice VIET )

Le démarreur VIET a bien d'autres utilisations que les opérations de démarrage dont il a été question plus haut (voir mise en marche du groupe moto-propulseur) parmi lesquelles:

- Remplissage automatique du réservoir à air du démarreur
- Remplissage automatique des réservoirs pour les servitudes de l'avion (commandes freins).
- Injections d'essence dans les tubulures d'admission
- Gonflage des pneus
- Remplissage des réservoirs de commande du canon et des mitrailleuses.

Le compresseur est utilisé pour remplir le réservoir des servitudes dont la capacité est proportionnée à son emploi et qui a pour but d'alimenter en air comprimé toutes les servitudes de l'avion.

Le compresseur effectue automatiquement la mise en pression totale et au sol d'un réservoir de 12 litres à 25 kg/cm2 en 7 minutes environ, au régime de 1200 tours moteurs.

**Fonctionnement à la main -**

S'assurer tout d'abord que le pointeau d'isolement du compresseur est fermé.

Mise en pression du réservoir du démarreur.

a) ouvrir le pointeau "Réservoir démarreur"

b) pomper jusqu'à la pression convenable 25 Kg.

c) fermer le pointeau "Réservoir démarreur"

**Gonflage des pneus -**

a) dévisser le bouchon de la prise de gonflage fixée sous le fuselage.

b) y fixer le tuyau souple et en relier l'autre extrémité au pneu à gonfler.

c) ouvrir à gauche et à fond le pointeau de gonflage et pomper jusqu'à la pression convenable (3,750 kg/cm2).

### English

**EQUIPMENT AND ARMAMENT -**

The principal items making up the equipment of the aircraft are:

The VIET starter, the OLAER brake system, the LEVY fire extinguisher, the MUNERELLE oxygen apparatus (inhaler), the O.T.C.537 radio (T.S.F.) set, the flight and navigation instruments, and the armament.

A - **VIET starter** ( see VIET manual )

Besides the starting operations dealt with above (see starting of the powerplant), the VIET starter has many other uses, among which:

- Automatic filling of the starter air reservoir
- Automatic filling of the reservoirs for the aircraft's services (brake controls).
- Fuel injection into the induction manifolds
- Inflation of the tyres
- Filling of the control reservoirs of the cannon and machine guns.

The compressor is used to fill the services reservoir, whose capacity is proportioned to its use and whose purpose is to supply compressed air to all the services of the aircraft.

On the ground, the compressor automatically brings a 12-litre reservoir up to its full pressure of 25 kg/cm2 in about 7 minutes, at an engine speed of 1200 rpm.

**Manual operation -**

First make sure that the compressor isolating needle valve is closed.

Pressurising the starter reservoir:

a) open the "Réservoir démarreur" (starter reservoir) needle valve

b) pump up to the proper pressure, 25 kg.

c) close the "Réservoir démarreur" needle valve

**Tyre inflation -**

a) unscrew the cap of the inflation connection fixed under the fuselage.

b) fit the flexible hose to it and connect the other end to the tyre to be inflated.

c) open the inflation needle valve fully to the left and pump up to the proper pressure (3.750 kg/cm2).

## Shock-absorber inflation; operation with the compressor; B - Brakes
*PDF page 44 · printed page 36*

### Français

**Gonflage des amortisseurs -**

La manoeuvre de mise en pression des amortisseurs d'atterrissage est identique à celle du gonflage des pneus (se conformer à la notice spéciale du constructeur "notice technique des amortisseurs OLAER").

**Fonctionnement avec compresseur -**

Mise en pression des réservoirs démarreur et servitude.

Le moteur étant en marche et la pression pour laquelle le régulateur est réglé, ouvrir les pointeaux "Réservoir démarreur" et "Isolement compresseur". Le régulateur limite la pression entre 25 et 30 Kg. suivant son réglage et met automatiquement le compresseur en relation avec l'atmosphère.

Les deux pointeaux étant ouverts, si la pression ne monte pas, provoquer une chute de pression supplémentaire à l'aide des purgeurs des réservoirs.

En cas d'arrêt prolongé du moteur et pour conserver la pression dans le réservoir du démarreur, fermer le pointeau "Réservoir démarreur".

**NOTA** : Le réservoir des servitudes est toujours en relation avec le compresseur.

a) la fermeture du pointeau "Isolement compresseur" isole la pompe à main et le réservoir du démarreur, du compresseur et du réservoir des servitudes.

b) la fermeture du pointeau "Réservoir démarreur" ferme totalement le réservoir du démarreur.

Pour faire communiquer les 2 réservoirs "Démarreur" et "Servitudes" ouvrir les 2 pointeaux "Isolement compresseur" et "Réservoir démarreur".

**Gonflage des pneus -**

Le moteur étant en marche, effectuer les opérations (a et b) indiquées pour le fonctionnement à la main.

Mettre en relation le réservoir des servitudes et le pneu à gonfler en ouvrant le pointeau "Isolement compresseur" ainsi que le pointeau de gonflage (ouvrir à gauche et à fond).

**Avis important** : Dans le cas où l'on fait usage, pour remplir le réservoir d'une bouteille d'air comprimé à haute pression, nous insistons tout particulièrement sur la nécessité d'interposer un appareil de sécurité taré à 25 ou 30 Kg. entre les réservoirs du démarreur et des servitudes et la bouteille d'air comprimé.

B - **FREINS** -

Les freins sont du type [blanc] -pneumatique, commandés par un bouton poussoir à la partie supérieure du manche à balai.

### English

**Shock-absorber inflation -**

The procedure for pressurising the landing-gear shock absorbers is identical to that for inflating the tyres (follow the manufacturer's special manual, "notice technique des amortisseurs OLAER" - technical manual for OLAER shock absorbers).

**Operation with the compressor -**

Pressurising the starter and services reservoirs.

With the engine running, and [at] the pressure for which the regulator is set, open the "Réservoir démarreur" (starter reservoir) and "Isolement compresseur" (compressor isolation) needle valves. The regulator limits the pressure to between 25 and 30 kg, according to its setting, and automatically connects the compressor to atmosphere.

With both needle valves open, if the pressure does not rise, create an additional pressure drop by means of the reservoir drain valves.

If the engine is to be stopped for a long time, and in order to keep the pressure in the starter reservoir, close the "Réservoir démarreur" needle valve.

**NOTE**: The services reservoir is always connected to the compressor.

a) closing the "Isolement compresseur" needle valve isolates the hand pump and the starter reservoir from the compressor and from the services reservoir.

b) closing the "Réservoir démarreur" needle valve shuts off the starter reservoir completely.

To connect the 2 reservoirs, "Démarreur" (starter) and "Servitudes" (services), open the 2 needle valves "Isolement compresseur" and "Réservoir démarreur".

**Tyre inflation -**

With the engine running, carry out operations (a and b) given for manual operation.

Connect the services reservoir to the tyre to be inflated by opening the "Isolement compresseur" needle valve as well as the inflation needle valve (open fully to the left).

**Important notice**: If a high-pressure compressed-air bottle is used to fill the reservoir, we particularly insist on the necessity of interposing a safety device set at 25 or 30 kg between the starter and services reservoirs and the compressed-air bottle.

B - **BRAKES** -

The brakes are of the [blank] -pneumatic type, controlled by a push button at the top of the control stick.

## Brakes (maintenance, operation); LEVY fire extinguisher; MUNERELLE oxygen apparatus; radio
*PDF page 45 · printed page 37*

### Français

**Entretien -**

Les mâchoires doivent avoir leurs garnitures sèches, la graisse empêchant complètement le fonctionnement du frein. Si les garnitures sont grasses, les nettoyer à l'essence ou mieux au benzol, au moyen d'une brosse métallique. De temps en temps il est bon de revoir toute l'installation des freins.

**Fonctionnement -**

Un détendeur ramène la pression d'air de la bouteille, 30 Kg à la pression d'utilisation 5 à 6 Kg. Un bouton placé sur le manche à balai permet au pilote d'envoyer de l'air comprimé dans le répartiteur-distributeur. Le pouce du pilote reçoit de ce bouton une réaction proportionnelle à la pression qui s'établit dans la canalisation, ce qui lui permet de sentir son freinage. Un levier conjugué avec le palonnier assure la répartition du freinage.

Quand le palonnier est en position médiane, la pression d'air envoyée dans le répartiteur est répartie également et les deux roues sont également freinées.

Quand on agit sur le palonnier pendant le freinage, la roue extérieure au virage se trouve diminuée proportionnellement à l'angle que l'on a donné au palonnier.

Il est rappelé que la pression d'utilisation des freins est comprise entre 5 et 6 Kg.

**Extincteur d'incendie LEVY** - se conformer à la notice "Extincteurs d'incendie LEVY".

**Inhalateur MUNERELLE** - (Pl 43)

Au moment de l'emploi c'est-à-dire lorsqu'on atteint l'altitude de 4.000 mètres, après s'être placé et avoir assujetti le masque sur son visage, ouvrir le robinet de l'inhalateur. Le débit, à partir de ce moment se règle automatiquement aux valeurs correctes suivant l'altitude.

Refermer le robinet de l'inhalateur dès qu'on est revenu à l'altitude où l'on n'a plus besoin d'oxygène. L'ouverture et la fermeture de ce robinet doivent se faire en vissant ou dévissant la manette en étoile à fond, dans le cas où le débit automatique d'oxygène viendrait à ne plus fonctionner, l'utilisateur à la ressource de pousser la manette de secours sur la position "DEBIT FORCE". La même manoeuvre peut dans des cas exceptionnels être pratiquée dans l'espoir de faire disparaître, par l'oxygène fourni en excès, des malaises survenant malgré l'emploi de l'inhalateur, dont le débit a été pourtant prévu pour répondre aux besoins organiques. Mais il importe que l'utilisateur ne perde pas de vue que cette manoeuvre épuise avec une trop grande rapidité sa provision d'oxygène et risque de le priver ainsi du secours de son inhalateur avant qu'il ne soit revenu aux altitudes inférieures à 4000 mètres (le débit de l'appareil en débit forcé est, en effet, de l'ordre de 500 litres/heure.

**Radiotélégraphie** - Radio-Industrie (Pl 44)

Le poste de T.S.F. permet au pilote de communiquer avec un autre avion ou avec un poste terrestre. Il se compose d'un émetteur et

### English

**Maintenance -**

The brake shoes must have their linings dry, as grease completely prevents the brake from working. If the linings are greasy, clean them with petrol or, better, with benzole, using a wire brush. From time to time it is advisable to go over the whole brake installation.

**Operation -**

A pressure-reducing valve reduces the air pressure of the bottle, 30 kg, to the working pressure of 5 to 6 kg. A button placed on the control stick enables the pilot to send compressed air into the distributor-proportioning valve. The pilot's thumb receives from this button a reaction proportional to the pressure established in the line, which enables him to feel his braking. A lever coupled to the rudder bar ensures the distribution of braking.

When the rudder bar is in the central position, the air pressure sent into the distributor is shared equally and both wheels are braked equally.

When the rudder bar is moved during braking, [the braking of] the wheel on the outside of the turn is reduced in proportion to the angle given to the rudder bar.

It is pointed out again that the working pressure of the brakes is between 5 and 6 kg.

**LEVY fire extinguisher** - follow the manual "Extincteurs d'incendie LEVY" (LEVY fire extinguishers).

**MUNERELLE oxygen apparatus (inhaler)** - (Pl 43)

At the time of use, that is on reaching an altitude of 4,000 metres, after having put on and secured the mask on one's face, open the cock of the oxygen apparatus. From that moment the flow regulates itself automatically to the correct values according to altitude.

Close the oxygen cock again as soon as one has come back to the altitude where oxygen is no longer needed. Opening and closing of this cock must be done by screwing or unscrewing the star-shaped handle fully home; should the automatic oxygen flow cease to function, the user has the resource of pushing the emergency lever to the "DEBIT FORCE" (forced flow) position. The same operation may, in exceptional cases, be carried out in the hope of relieving, by the excess oxygen supplied, discomfort occurring in spite of the use of the apparatus, whose flow has nevertheless been designed to meet physiological needs. But it is important that the user should not lose sight of the fact that this operation exhausts his oxygen supply far too quickly and risks depriving him of the help of his apparatus before he has come back to altitudes below 4000 metres (the flow of the apparatus in forced-flow mode is in fact of the order of 500 litres/hour.

**Radio (wireless telegraphy)** - Radio-Industrie (Pl 44)

The radio (T.S.F.) set enables the pilot to communicate with another aircraft or with a ground station. It consists of a transmitter and

de deux récepteurs; l'antenne d'émission est fixée et située sur la partie supérieure du fuselage. L'antenne de réception relevable est sous le fuselage. La suspension élastique des antennes et du poste permet d'exécuter en toute sécurité les missions radio à tous les régimes du moteur.

## Ground handling (continued) — Ground towing — Parking without shelter
*PDF page 49 · printed page 41*

### Français

chariot quatre hommes soulèvent l'avion par la barre de levage, les autres ne font que pousser ou tirer.

NOTA- Ne jamais prendre appui sur le bord de fuite de l'aile et en particulier sur les ailerons ni sur les volets de courbure.

## Remorquage au sol

Pour tous les déplacements de l'avion, immobiliser les gouvernes par le dispositif de blocage des commandes (Pl.54).

## Remorquage par l'avant

On fixe deux gros câbles autour de la partie inférieure des amortisseurs. Ces deux câbles sont maintenus écartés par une barre rigide et l'attelage est complété par deux chaînes obliques qui viennent s'accrocher au tracteur (Pl.53) (tracteur à chenille ou ordinaire).

## Remorquage par l'arrière

On place la béquille sur le chariot porte-queue, qui est muni d'un godet. La béquille est, de plus, fixée dans le godet de façon à appliquer le corps de béquille et à ne pas faire travailler l'amortisseur.

Ce godet peut pivoter est ensuite tiré par un tracteur. Ce mode de remorquage est à utiliser pour un trajet assez long sur une piste non cimentée.

NOTA- **NE JAMAIS FAIRE RECULER L'APPAREIL AVEC LA BEQUILLE AU SOL**

## Campement sans abri

1°/ Dispositif d'amarrage au sol (Pl.55)

Après avoir ouvert la porte de visite (intrados) donnant accès à la ferrure de levage fixée sur le longeron, accrocher un palan ou un câble à tendeur à l'anneau d'amarrage de la ferrure. Planter des tirefonds et y passer l'autre extrémité du câble ou du palan. Tendre.

En cas d'impossibilité de planter les tirefonds on amarrera les câbles à l'aide d'objets lourds en ayant soin de tourner l'appareil vent arrière pour que le vent le plaque au sol.

On amarrera le fuselage par un dispositif analogue en passant un câble ou un tube dans le trou de levage percé à la partie arrière.

De toute façon, on immobilisera les gouvernes avec le dispositif consistant en un capuchon et 4 bras souples en câble d'acier. Coiffer le manche à balai avec le capuchon et accrocher les deux mousquetons des câbles sans tendeurs au cale-pieds du palonnier. Tirer le manche le plus en arrière possible, accrocher les deux mousquetons qui restent aux anneaux prévus sur les lisses au niveau du siège. Serrer les tendeurs.

### English

trolley, four men lift the aircraft by the lifting bar; the others merely push or pull.

NOTE- Never bear on the trailing edge of the wing, and in particular never on the ailerons or the flaps.

## Ground towing

For all movements of the aircraft, immobilise the control surfaces with the control lock device (Pl.54).

## Towing from the front

Two heavy cables are fastened around the lower part of the shock absorbers (oleo struts). These two cables are held apart by a rigid bar, and the hitch is completed by two oblique chains which are hooked to the tractor (Pl.53) (tracked or ordinary tractor).

## Towing from the rear

The tail skid is placed on the tail trolley, which is fitted with a cup (socket). The tail skid is, moreover, secured in the cup so as to bear on the skid body and not to load the shock absorber.

This cup can pivot [and] is then pulled by a tractor. This method of towing is to be used for a fairly long journey on an unpaved (non-concrete) strip.

NOTE- **NEVER MOVE THE AIRCRAFT BACKWARDS WITH THE TAIL SKID ON THE GROUND**

## Parking without shelter

1°/ Ground tie-down (mooring) device (Pl.55)

After opening the inspection door (lower surface) giving access to the hoisting fitting attached to the spar, hook a hoist (block and tackle) or a cable with turnbuckle to the mooring ring of the fitting. Drive in ground screw-pickets and pass the other end of the cable or hoist through them. Tension.

If it is impossible to drive in the screw-pickets, the cables will be anchored by means of heavy objects, taking care to turn the aircraft tail into wind so that the wind presses it onto the ground.

The fuselage will be tied down by a similar device, passing a cable or a tube through the hoisting hole drilled in the rear part.

In any case, the control surfaces will be immobilised with the device consisting of a cap and 4 flexible arms of steel cable. Fit the cap over the control stick and hook the two snap-hooks of the cables without turnbuckles to the foot-rests (toe-clips) of the rudder bar. Pull the stick as far back as possible, hook the two remaining snap-hooks to the rings provided on the stringers at seat level. Tighten the turnbuckles.

## Cover — Parking under regulation shelters
*PDF page 50 · printed page 42*

### Français

## Housse -

L'avion étant en conduite intérieure et le moteur recouvert d'un capot, il n'est pas nécessaire de mettre une housse pour un court séjour sur le terrain. Mais, par mesure de précaution il est bon de recouvrir le moteur et l'habitacle pour les longs campements sans abri.

## Campement sous les abris réglementaires (Pl 56)

L'abri pouvant recevoir un avion monté doit avoir au minimum :

longueur : 7,50 m. - largeur : 12,50 - hauteur : 3,80 m.

Pour les dimensions générales de l'avion, consulter les trois vues.

Les hangars réglementaires peuvent contenir :

| Abri | Capacité |
|---|---|
| hangars métalliques démontables 9 x 10 m | 1 seul avion. |
| tentes - abris A-16 - 16 x 12 | 2 avions. |
| hangars bessonneau 20 x 28 | 5 avions. |
| hangars métalliques démontables 20x30 | 8 avions. |
| hangars bessonneau 26 x 28 | 12 avions. |

### English

## Cover -

Since the aircraft has an enclosed cockpit (canopy) and the engine is covered by a cowling, it is not necessary to fit a cover for a short stay on the airfield. But, as a precaution, it is advisable to cover the engine and the cockpit for long periods of parking without shelter.

## Parking under regulation shelters (Pl 56)

A shelter able to accommodate an assembled aircraft must have at least:

length: 7.50 m - width: 12.50 - height: 3.80 m.

For the general dimensions of the aircraft, consult the three-view drawing.

The regulation hangars can hold:

| Shelter | Capacity |
|---|---|
| dismountable metal hangars 9 x 10 m | 1 aircraft only. |
| tents - shelters A-16 - 16 x 12 | 2 aircraft. |
| Bessonneau hangars 20 x 28 | 5 aircraft. |
| dismountable metal hangars 20x30 | 8 aircraft. |
| Bessonneau hangars 26 x 28 | 12 aircraft. |

## Preparation and inspection — Before flight — 1°/ Airframe
*PDF page 51 · printed page 43*

### Français

# PREPARATION ET VERIFICATION

## AVANT LE VOL -

La vérification de l'avion doit être effectuée avant chaque vol et pour être efficace et rapide, faite dans l'ordre suivant;

### 1°/ Planeur -

a) Cellule, fuselage, empennages -

Visite d'ensemble du rivetage et des panneaux de revêtement pour en vérifier le bon état. Vérification de la fermeture des portes de visites et de la fixation correcte des carénages.

Vérification de la facilité le largage du capot pilote.

**Défauts** -
Perforation possible des tôles au cours des manutentions de l'avion. Oubli d'une corde à piano de fermeture ou de vis de fixation.

b) Ailerons, gouvernes, commandes de vol -

Vérification du fonctionnement correct des gouvernes, des bielles et articulations des commandes, des amortisseurs d'aileron et d'empennages.

**Défauts** -
Présence d'un corps étranger ou d'un outil dans les articulations graissage insuffisant.

c) Volets de courbure.

Vérification du fonctionnement des bielles et articulations vérin.

**Défauts** -
Pièce faussée, mauvais graissage.

d) Train :

Vérifier fixation des panneaux, gonflement des amortisseurs.

**Défauts** : amortisseurs dégonflés.

**Béquille** -
mêmes précautions que pour le train.

### English

# PREPARATION AND INSPECTION

## BEFORE FLIGHT -

The inspection of the aircraft must be carried out before every flight and, to be effective and quick, done in the following order;

### 1°/ Airframe -

a) Structure (cellule), fuselage, tail unit -

General inspection of the riveting and of the skin panels to check that they are in good condition. Check that the inspection doors are closed and that the fairings are correctly fastened.

Check the ease of jettisoning of the pilot's canopy (capot pilote).

**Defects** -
Possible perforation of the skin sheets during handling of the aircraft. A piano-wire closing pin or a fastening screw left out (forgotten).

b) Ailerons, control surfaces, flight controls -

Check the correct operation of the control surfaces, of the control rods and joints (hinges) of the controls, and of the aileron and tail-unit dampers.

**Defects** -
Presence of a foreign body or a tool in the joints; insufficient lubrication.

c) Flaps.

Check the operation of the rods and joints [and] the actuator.

**Defects** -
Bent (distorted) part, poor lubrication.

d) Landing gear:

Check fastening of the panels, inflation of the shock absorbers (oleo struts).

**Defects**: deflated shock absorbers.

**Tail skid** -
same precautions as for the landing gear.

A faint handwritten tick/mark near the 'D.520.S.' header. 'Archives départementales' stamp.*

## Before flight — 2°/ Powerplant
*PDF page 52 · printed page 44*

### Français

### 2°/ Groupe motopropulseur

a) **Circulation d'essence** -

Vérification de l'étanchéité des carburateurs et de la canalisation, des joints des tuyauteries d'admission.

Vérification du plein d'essence et du serrage des bouchons.

**Défauts** -
Mauvais montage, fuites aux réservoirs ou aux tuyauteries, plein incomplet, mauvaise fermeture des bouchons.

b) **Circulation d'huile** -

Vérification du plein, mise en place correcte du bouchon du réservoir. Etanchéité du réservoir, des canalisations et des radiateurs.

**Défauts** -
Mauvaise fermeture du bouchon. Plein incomplet - fuites.

c) **Circulation du liquide réfrigérant** -

Vérification du plein. Serrage et freinage des bouchons témoins de remplissage. Serrage et freinage du bouchon de remplissage du radiateur. Vérification de l'étanchéité du radiateur, tuyauteries et raccords.

**Défauts** -
Manque de liquide dû à des fuites du radiateur ou des tuyauteries - Mauvaise fixation.

d) **Allumage** -

Vérification du système de fixation des magnétos et des connexions

**Défauts** -
Desserrage du système de fixation - Court-circuit.

e) **Commandes moteur** -

Vérification du fonctionnement des commandes des gaz et de correction altimétrique, au poste de pilotage, ainsi que de la manoeuvre du robinet conjugué d'essence et d'huile et des commandes d'amorçage des pompes A.M.

Vérification du blocage des contre-écrous sur les coulisseaux de traversée de cloison pare-feu des commandes des gaz et de correction.

**Défauts** -
Coincement ou jeu excessif. Desserrage des contre-écrous.

f) **Hélice** -

Vérification de l'état de l'hélice et du système de changement de pas.

**Défauts** -
Détérioration accidentelle de l'hélice.

### English

### 2°/ Powerplant

a) **Fuel system** -

Check the tightness (absence of leaks) of the carburettors and of the piping, and of the joints of the induction (intake) pipes.

Check that the fuel tanks are full and that the filler caps are tight.

**Defects** -
Incorrect assembly, leaks at the tanks or in the pipes, tanks not completely filled, filler caps badly closed.

b) **Oil system** -

Check that the tank is full and that the tank cap is correctly fitted. Tightness of the tank, of the pipes and of the [oil] radiators.

**Defects** -
Cap badly closed. Tank not completely filled - leaks.

c) **Coolant system** -

Check that it is full. Tightening and locking (wire-locking) of the filling level-check plugs. Tightening and locking of the radiator filler cap. Check the tightness of the radiator, pipes and unions.

**Defects** -
Lack of liquid due to leaks from the radiator or the pipes - Faulty fastening.

d) **Ignition** -

Check the magneto fastening system and the connections

**Defects** -
Loosening of the fastening system - Short circuit.

e) **Engine controls** -

Check the operation of the throttle and altitude mixture control (correction altimétrique) controls in the cockpit, as well as the operation of the combined fuel-and-oil cock and of the priming controls of the A.M. pumps.

Check the locking of the lock-nuts on the sliding guides where the throttle and mixture-correction controls pass through the firewall.

**Defects** -
Jamming or excessive play. Loosening of the lock-nuts.

f) **Propeller** -

Check the condition of the propeller and of the pitch-change system.

**Defects** -
Accidental damage to the propeller.

*Notes: Clear page. Section headings a)-f) are underlined in the original (rendered bold). 'pompes A.M.' left as abbreviation (not expanded on the page; possibly hand-operated/'à main' pumps, not confirmed). Last word of 'commandes d'amorçage des pompes' runs into the right margin but is legible. Ink stroke over 'de changement' near bottom (scan/handling mark, not a correction).*

## Before flight — 3°/ Equipment (starter, brakes, fire extinguisher, oxygen apparatus)
*PDF page 53 · printed page 45*

### Français

### 3°/ Equipement -

a) **Démarreur** -

**Pompe à air** -

Si au cours de la manoeuvre de la pompe, le levier abandonné à lui-même change rapidement de position, l'un des clapets porte mal sur son siège.

Essayer de le remettre en place en agitant rapidement le levier dans les deux sens. Si cette opération n'arrête pas le déplacement du levier il y a lieu de nettoyer les clapets de haute et basse pression. Les sortir de la pompe et les laver dans l'essence, le benzol ou l'alcool sans séparer leurs pièces.

Veiller au remontage à l'étanchéité des joints.

**Pulvérisateur** -

Si la clé de distribution devient dure, frapper légèrement en bout avec un manche en bois.

Si elle devient trop libre, débloquer l'écrou du boulon, enlever la goupille fendue qui arrête le bouchon fileté vissé en bout de la clé.

Visser légèrement ce dernier et remplacer la goupille fendue en cherchant deux trous en regard.

Resserrer l'écrou de la manette. Veiller au serrage du presse-étoupe du pointeau de démarrage.

**Pompe d'injection** -

Si le piston de la pompe d'injection devient sec par suite du passage dans l'essence, le graisser avec de l'huile de ricin ou de la glycérine, en remplissant le godet de l'écrou-guide. Le faire fonctionner plusieurs fois afin de faire descendre l'huile dans la garniture. Cette opération peut être faite aussi souvent que possible.

b) **Freins** -

Vérifications des canalisations et des raccords, des bagues, des roues (qui ne doivent avoir qu'un jeu très faible). Les mâchoires de freins doivent être très propres.

c) **Extincteur** -

Vérifications de la ligne de détecteurs.

**Inhalateur** - (Pl.43)

Vérification de la quantité d'oxygène emportée et du bon fonctionnement de l'appareil. La quantité d'oxygène que nécessitera la mission aérienne projetée est directement fonction de la durée de l'ascension au-dessous de 4000 m. et des altitudes à atteindre. Vérification de la position fermée du robinet de l'inhalateur et de la manette de secours sur "DEBIT AUTOMATIQUE".

### English

### 3°/ Equipment -

a) **Starter** -

**Air pump** -

If, while the pump is being operated, the lever, when let go, rapidly changes position, one of the valves is not seating properly.

Try to reseat it by rapidly working the lever in both directions. If this does not stop the lever from moving, the high- and low-pressure valves must be cleaned. Remove them from the pump and wash them in petrol, benzol or alcohol without separating their parts.

On reassembly, make sure the joints (seals) are tight.

**Sprayer (distributor)** -

If the distribution key (cock) becomes stiff, tap it lightly on the end with a wooden handle.

If it becomes too free, slacken the nut of the bolt and remove the split pin that retains the threaded plug screwed into the end of the key.

Screw the latter in slightly and refit the split pin, finding two holes that line up.

Retighten the nut of the handle. Make sure the gland (stuffing box) of the starting needle valve is tight.

**Injection (priming) pump** -

If the piston of the injection pump becomes dry as a result of the passage of petrol, lubricate it with castor oil or glycerine by filling the cup of the guide nut. Operate it several times so as to make the oil run down into the packing. This operation may be done as often as possible.

b) **Brakes** -

Check the pipes and unions, the rings (bushes), and the wheels (which must have only very slight play). The brake shoes must be very clean.

c) **Fire extinguisher** -

Check the detector line.

**Oxygen apparatus (inhaler)** - (Pl.43)

Check the quantity of oxygen carried and the correct functioning of the apparatus. The quantity of oxygen that the planned air mission will require depends directly on the duration of the climb below 4000 m and on the altitudes to be reached. Check that the oxygen-apparatus cock is in the closed position and that the emergency lever is on "DEBIT AUTOMATIQUE" (AUTOMATIC FLOW).

## Before flight — Equipment (continued) — 4°/ Armament
*PDF page 54 · printed page 46*

### Français

e) **Instruments de bord et de navigation, de contrôle moteur** -

Vérification du parfait état de fonctionnement, de la propreté des cadrans et des verres.

f) **Instruments électriques, éclairage** -

Bonne fixation et charge de la batterie. Bon fonctionnement des feux de bord, de route et de signalisation, état des lampes.

Au cours d'un point fixe du moteur, contrôle de la dynamo (débit en charge).

g) **Chauffage** -

Dans le cas de marche sans batterie, la vérification s'effectue au cours d'un essai au point fixe.

h) **Déclenchement des pots éclairants** -

Se conformer à la notice du fabricant.

### 4°/ Armement -

Vérifier le gonflage de la bouteille.

a) **Canon** -

Vérification du chargeur (celui-ci peut contenir 60 obus) du bon état des parois de la boite de récupération des étuis. (une déformation pouvant amener un arrêt au glissement des douilles).

Pression de la bouteille d'air : 25 à 30 Kg.

Vérification du bon fonctionnement des sélecteurs sécurité, armement, détente et de la poignée de détente sur manche à balai.

**Défauts** - Pression insuffisante.

Mauvais fonctionnement des commandes par suite du manque d'étanchéité. Mauvais état des clapets et des joints.

b) **Mitrailleuses** - (Pression de la bouteille 25 à 30 Kg).

Vérification de la fixation des armes et du fonctionnement des commandes ~~pneumatiques~~. Les chargeurs peuvent contenir 650 cartouches chacun.

**Défauts** - (Les mêmes que pour le canon.)

c) **Viseur O.P.L.** (Pl.45).

Vérification de la fixation, du fonctionnement, de l'alimentation Présence du boitier de rechange. Propreté des glaces.

### English

e) **Flight, navigation and engine-monitoring instruments** -

Check that they are in perfect working order, and that the dials and glasses are clean.

f) **Electrical instruments, lighting** -

Battery securely fastened and charged. Correct operation of the cockpit (instrument) lights, navigation lights and signal lights; condition of the lamps.

During an engine ground run (point fixe), check the dynamo (charging output).

g) **Heating** -

When operating without a battery, the check is made during a ground-run test.

h) **Release of the flares (pots éclairants)** -

Follow the manufacturer's instructions.

### 4°/ Armament -

Check the inflation (charge) of the [air] bottle.

a) **Cannon** -

Check the magazine (which can hold 60 shells), and the good condition of the walls of the spent-case collector box (a deformation could stop the cases from sliding).

Air bottle pressure: 25 to 30 kg [/cm²].

Check the correct operation of the safety, cocking (arming) and firing selectors and of the firing grip (trigger) on the control stick.

**Defects** - Insufficient pressure.

Faulty operation of the controls owing to lack of tightness (leaks). Poor condition of the valves and seals.

b) **Machine guns** - (Bottle pressure 25 to 30 kg [/cm²]).

Check the fastening of the guns and the operation of the ~~pneumatic~~ controls. The magazines can each hold 650 rounds.

**Defects** - (The same as for the cannon.)

c) **O.P.L. gunsight** (Pl.45).

Check its fastening, its operation, its [electrical] supply. Presence of the spare [lamp] box. Cleanliness of the glasses.

*PDF page 55 · printed page 47*

### Français

e) **Lance-bombes éclairantes** - (voir la notice du fabricant).

f) **Radiotélégraphie** -

Vérification du fonctionnement et de la fixation par un spécialiste en temps voulu et, en dernier ressort, de la fixation de l'antenne d'émission et du système de relevage de l'antenne de réception.

Fixation - Etats des glaces - propreté.

## APRES LE VOL -

Après chaque vol, le mécanicien note sur les documents en vigueur, les observations éventuelles formulées par le pilote sur les incidents survenus au cours du vol.

### 1°/ Planeur -

a) **Cellule** -

Vérification du revêtement et du rivetage.

**Défauts** - Perforations, plissements, déformations.

Un plissement de tôle indique une déformation permanente et nécessite une vérification sérieuse de la partie incriminée.

b) **Ailerons** -

Vérification du revêtement, des articulations.

**Défauts** - Jeu, perte des freins sur axes et guignol,

### English

e) **Flare launcher** - (see the manufacturer's instructions).

f) **Radio (wireless telegraphy)** -

Check of operation and fastening by a specialist in due course and, as a last resort, [check] of the fastening of the transmitting antenna and of the raising (retraction) system of the receiving antenna.

g) **Pilot's seat** -

Examination of the seat, its fastening and operation.

**Defects** - Faulty fastening, wear of the control cable.

h) **Windscreen** -

Fastening - Condition of the glass panes - cleanliness.

i) **Parachute** -

Secure fastening of the attachment bar (clip) of the automatic-release (static-line) cable.

## AFTER FLIGHT -

After every flight, the mechanic records in the current documents any remarks made by the pilot on incidents that occurred during the flight.

### 1°/ Airframe -

a) **Structure (cellule)** -

Check of the skin and of the riveting.

**Defects** - Perforations, wrinkling (buckling), deformations.

A wrinkle in the sheet metal indicates a permanent deformation and calls for a thorough check of the part concerned.

b) **Ailerons** -

Check of the skin [covering] and of the hinges (joints).

**Defects** - Play, loss of the locking (safety) on the pins and on the control horn (guignol),

*PDF page 56 · printed page 48*

### Français

c) **Fuselage** -

Vérification du revêtement et du rivetage.

d) **Empennages** -

Vérification du revêtement, du rivetage des ferrures d'assemblages au fuselage.

f) **Commandes de vol** -

Vérification du fonctionnement, du blocage des contre-écrous des embouts filetés, des roulements d'articulations des renvois de la tension des câbles du freinage, du goupillage des axes, du nettoyage des poulies ou axes d'articulation, s'il y a lieu.

g) **Train d'atterrissage** -

1°/ **Béquille** -

Vérifications du réglage de l'amortisseur.

### 2°/ Groupe motopropulseur -

D'une façon générale, vérification des capotages et surveillance de l'absence de criques.

a) **Moteur** -

c) **Fuselage** -

Check of the skin and of the riveting.

**Defects** - (The same as for the structure [cellule]).

d) **Tail unit** -

Check of the skin, and of the riveting of the assembly fittings to the fuselage.

**Defects** - Play in the attachment pins.

e) **Control surfaces** -

Checks of the fabric covering, of the hinges (joints) and of the control horns.

**Defects** -
Tears in the fabric, excessive play or jamming due to deformation of a component.

f) **Flight controls** -

Check of the operation, of the locking of the lock-nuts of the threaded end-fittings, of the bearings of the joints of the bellcranks (relays), of the tension of the cables, of the locking (safety wiring), of the pinning (split pins) of the pins (bolts), and of the cleanliness of the pulleys or hinge pins, if necessary.

**Defects** - Loss of a locking device. Loosening, wear of a cable, play in the fastening of the end-fittings of control rods.

g) **Landing gear** -

Check the operation and the attachments.

1°/ **Tail skid** -

Checks of the adjustment of the shock absorber.

**Defects** - Shock absorber out of adjustment, shock-absorber rod bent.

### 2°/ Powerplant -

In general, check of the cowlings and watch for the absence of cracks.

a) **Engine** -

Removal of the upper side cowling panels, allowing inspection of the engine components (see the instructions in the HISPANO SUIZA 12 Y 45 engine manual).

Check of the engine mount, of the fastenings; cleaning of the engine compartment.

## After flight — Powerplant (continued) — 3°/ Equipment

### Français

b) **Commandes moteur** -

Vérification du fonctionnement, du réglage, du blocage des contre-écrous et rotules, du goupillage.

c) **Circulation d'essence** -

Vérification de la fixation des réservoirs, du serrage des sangles, des tôles de capotage, de l'étanchéité des réservoirs, du freinage des écrous, des raccords, examen des tuyauteries souples.

**Défauts** - Fuite ou desserrage des raccords.

d) **Circulation d'huile** -

Fixation du réservoir, étanchéité des radiateurs. Vérification des raccords, des tuyauteries.

**Défauts** - Fuites, desserrage des raccords et des colliers.

e) **Circulation du liquide réfrigérant** -

Vérification des tuyauteries et des joints.

**Défauts** - Fuites, colliers desserrés.

f) **Hélice**.-

Vérification soigneuse du système de changement de pas - propreté.

**Défauts** - Eclats sur l'hélice, jeu du moyeu d'hélice sur le plateau du moteur.

### 3°/ Equipement -

a) **Démarreur** -

Vérification de la fixation, du gonflage du réservoir d'air, du bon état et de la fixation des tuyauteries.

**Défauts** - Desserrage des supports - fuites ou tuyauteries abimées.

b) **Freins** -

Vérification de l'étanchéité du circuit et des raccords, éventuellement, en cas d'observations du pilote, vérification détaillée des organes incriminés.

c) **Extincteur** -

Vérification du bon état de l'extincteur (qu'il n'y ait pas eu fonctionnement inopiné), de la pression dans le réservoir de liquide; fixation du réservoir et de la rampe d'injection..

Nettoyage des orifices de sortie du liquide.

Examen de la canalisation des détecteurs.

### English

b) **Engine controls** -

Check of the operation, of the adjustment, of the locking of the lock-nuts and ball joints, and of the pinning (split pins).

c) **Fuel system** -

Check of the fastening of the tanks, of the tightness of the straps, of the cowling sheets, of the tightness (leak-freeness) of the tanks, of the locking of the nuts, of the unions; examination of the flexible pipes.

**Defects** - Leaking or loosening of the unions.

d) **Oil system** -

Fastening of the tank, tightness of the radiators. Check of the unions and of the pipes.

**Defects** - Leaks, loosening of the unions and of the clips (hose clamps).

e) **Coolant system** -

Check of the pipes and of the joints.

**Defects** - Leaks, loose clips (hose clamps).

f) **Propeller**.-

Careful check of the pitch-change system - cleanliness.

**Defects** - Chips (splinters) on the propeller, play of the propeller hub on the engine [drive] plate (flange).

### 3°/ Equipment -

a) **Starter** -

Check of the fastening, of the inflation (pressure) of the air reservoir, and of the condition and fastening of the pipes.

**Defects** - Loosening of the supports - leaks or damaged pipes.

b) **Brakes** -

Check of the tightness of the circuit and of the unions; if necessary, in case of remarks by the pilot, detailed check of the components concerned.

c) **Fire extinguisher** -

Check of the good condition of the extinguisher (that it has not been accidentally discharged), of the pressure in the liquid reservoir; fastening of the reservoir and of the injection ramp (spray manifold).

Cleaning of the liquid outlet orifices.

Examination of the detector line (piping).

*Notes: Clear page. Double full stop after 'rampe d'injection..' in original. Small pencil marks in header (near 'NOTICE DESCRIPTIVE') and at left margin. 'Archives départementales' stamp at top left.*

---

## After flight — Equipment (end) — 4°/ Armament — Corrosion prevention
*PDF page 58 · printed page 50*

### Français

**Défauts** - Canalisation coupée - fuites - Resserrage des supports.

d) **Inhalateur** -

Contrôle de fermeture du pointeau de la bouteille d'oxygène et de la fixation de celle-ci, de l'état des canalisations et de l'inhalateur proprement dit.

Mise en place du masque et de la tuyauterie souple.

e) **Instruments de bord, de navigation, de contrôle moteur** -

Mêmes vérifications qu'avant le vol.

f) **Installation électrique**.-

Débranchement et démontage de la batterie d'accumulateurs de sur son support, s'il y a lieu.

Vérification, au préalable, de tous les circuits, connexions de la fixation et de l'état des conducteurs (dans la mesure du possible)

Ne pas omettre, en cas de débranchement d'un conducteur de fixer celui-ci sur une borne d'attente.

**Défauts** - Lampes grillées - mauvais contact - fusibles brulés.

**Pots et bombes éclairantes** - (voir la notice du fabricant).

### 4°/ Armements -

a ) position des armes à "Sécurité" enlèvement aussitôt après le vol, des obus et des cartouches n'ayant pas été utilisés: examen des armes (qu'elles soient vides).

Vérification de la fixation des armes, de leur commande et des boites à cartouches.

Pour l'entretien des armes, voir documents spéciaux.

b) **Radiotélégraphie** -

Vérification et entretien effectués par un spécialiste.

## Pour éviter la corrosion

Les tôles en duralumin sont recouvertes sur les deux faces d'un vernis incolore.

### English

**Defects** - Severed line (piping) - leaks - Retightening of the supports.

d) **Oxygen apparatus (inhaler)** -

Check that the needle valve of the oxygen bottle is closed and that the bottle is securely fastened; check the condition of the lines and of the oxygen apparatus itself.

Fitting of the mask and of the flexible hose.

e) **Flight, navigation and engine-monitoring instruments** -

Same checks as before flight.

f) **Electrical installation**.-

Disconnection and removal of the storage battery from its support, if necessary.

Prior check of all the circuits, connections, fastening and condition of the conductors (wires) (as far as possible)

Do not forget, when a conductor is disconnected, to secure it to a standby (parking) terminal.

**Defects** - Burnt-out lamps - poor contact - blown fuses.

**Flares and flare bombs** - (see the manufacturer's instructions).

### 4°/ Armament -

a) Set the weapons to "Sécurité" (SAFE); remove, immediately after the flight, any shells and cartridges not used; examine the weapons (that they are empty).

Check the fastening of the weapons, of their controls and of the cartridge boxes.

For the maintenance of the weapons, see the special documents.

b) **Radio (wireless telegraphy)** -

Checking and maintenance carried out by a specialist.

## To prevent corrosion

The duralumin sheets are coated on both faces with a colourless varnish.

*Notes: Clear page. Item 'a )' under 4°/ Armements is typed far to the right of the line (indented into a gap). 'documents spéciaux' partly obscured by an ink smudge but readable. 'de sur son support' is original wording. Corrosion heading underlined. Pencil strokes at lower centre. 'Archives départementales' stamp top right.*

Stamp: ARCHIVES … DÉPARTEMENTALES [partly legible]

> Three-view general arrangement of the D.520 at 1/50 scale on a single fold-out sheet. Top left: front view with the wing dihedral, the under-fuselage radiator bath, and the main undercarriage legs and wheels drawn dashed (extended), dimensioned for track and overall span. Bottom left: plan view (nose pointing to the bottom of the sheet, tail at the top), showing the rounded wingtips, the straight trailing edge carrying aileron and flap, the cockpit/canopy, the two retracted main wheels drawn as dashed circles just ahead of the wing root, and the tailplane with its span dimension. Top right: side view in flying attitude standing on the extended main wheel (wheel dashed), with three-blade propeller, canopy, ventral radiator and fin/rudder, dimensioned for total height, propeller-axis height and propeller ground clearance; the overall-length dimension line is drawn but carries no figure.

*Notes: Height figure at far left of the side view is small and blurred: read as '3m435' (the digit after 'm' looks like 4) but '3m335' cannot be excluded. Propeller-axis height reads '1m950' (vertical text, blurred) and differs from the 1.84 m given for the flying-line attitude in the Fiche des cotes générales (PDF p.63); the two may refer to different attitudes/conditions. Plate number written 'PL_0' (Plate 0). Archive stamp ('ARCHIVES … DÉPARTEMENTALES' with a date) and handwritten folio '53' at lower right. Signature/date lower left 'E. DEFFES 21-5-40'.
Audit fix: height label re-examined on the rotated, enlarged green-channel scan: the digit after 'm' is drawn as a diagonal + crossbar + vertical stem (a 4), unlike the two 3s on either side, so '3m435' is retained and '3m335' rejected; confidence raised low -> medium. Track '2m830' also re-checked: the middle digit has an open left side (a 3), unlike the closed '8' before it, so 2m830 (not 2m880) is confirmed.*

> General arrangement of the left half-wing (tip at left, root cut at right). At the top is a front elevation of the half-wing showing the upper and lower surface lines with the 22 rib stations numbered in circles from the tip (1) to the root (22), annotated with the slopes 'tangente 0.0046' (upper surface, inboard part) and 'tangente 0.1246' (lower surface). Below is the plan view of the structure with the leading edge at the top: a single wide main spar running straight from rib 1 to the root, ribs at each station, a rear (false) spar carrying the aileron (outboard) and flap (inboard), lettered call-outs A-F to the ailerons, aileron control, inspection doors, flare, machine guns and flaps, and the circular wheel well ('Passage de la roue') ahead of the spar at the root. At the right is a root aerofoil section drawn vertically (leading edge at top) showing the spar, a semicircular recess in the lower surface near the leading edge, and the flap housed under the trailing edge.

## Wing structure
*PDF page 69 · Pl. 2*

### Français

D.520 S
55 (handwritten folio)

> Perspective cutaway drawing (no call-outs) of a half-wing seen from the root end, from slightly above and ahead, with the tip at upper right. The skin is cut away in jagged edges to reveal the structure: a leading-edge (nose) section built of nose ribs pierced with flanged circular lightening holes and vertical stiffening beads; a single deep main spar with a wide top flange, vertical web stiffeners and rectangular lightening cut-outs in its web, with rivet rows along the flanges; and an aft box of full-depth ribs with large flanged lightening holes ending at a rear (false) spar. Behind the rear spar the trailing-edge control surface (aileron/flap) is shown with its own small ribs and a hinge bracket below the rear spar. The upper skin shows spanwise rows of rivets (stringer/rib lines) running to a rounded wingtip.

*Notes: No dimensions or call-outs on this plate. Handwritten folio '55' and archive stamp lower right.*

## Wing spar
*PDF page 70 · Pl. 3*

### Français

PL. N° 3
LONGERON D'AILE
56 (folio manuscrit)
Tampon : ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES

### English

PL. No. 3
WING SPAR
56 (handwritten folio)
Stamp: ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES

> Shaded elevation (seen from the front, in slight perspective) of one half of the main wing spar, from the tip at left to the root/centre end at right, with no call-outs. The spar is a riveted I-beam: upper and lower flanges (booms) with rows of rivets, a thin web shaded with fine vertical lines, and vertical angle stiffeners at regular intervals (doubled at some stations). Depth increases steadily from the pointed tip to the root. About midway the spar shows a change of slope where a heavy bolted fitting with a large circular boss is attached; just inboard of it is a Y-shaped forged fitting on the web. At the root end the flanges thicken (drawn solid) and project as end caps for the centre joint, and small lugs stand on the upper flange; a riveted doubler/fairing is shown along the lower flange near the root.

*Notes: No dimensions or labels other than the title. Identification of the fittings is an inference. Handwritten folio '56' and archive stamp lower right.*

> Shaded perspective cutaway (no call-outs) of the joint at the aircraft centreline between the two halves of the main wing spar. The upper and lower spar booms are built up of stacked flat laminations (shown hatched in section) whose ends are stepped in interlocking 'castellated' tongues where the two half-spars meet. A large elongated splice (cover) plate with many bolts covers the upper boom joint and a smaller tapered splice plate the lower boom joint; a bolted eye-lug fitting stands on top of the upper splice plate. The spar web is shown as a sheet with built-up vertical end stiffeners/channels at the joint, partly cut away to show the doubled web and riveted angles.

*Notes: No dimensions. Draughtsman's signature at lower left is very faint (read 'R. Maulis', uncertain). Handwritten folio '57' and archive stamp lower right.*

The flap has a rounded, skinned nose ahead of a channel-section spar; aft of the spar, pressed ribs alternate with diagonal braces forming a triangulated (Warren-type) framework to a sharp trailing edge; the near end rib is a closed plate with a slotted lightening cut-out. Three hinge brackets project from the lower front of the nose at the near end, the middle and the far end, each with a dark curved band wrapped round the nose at the same station (hinge straps/fittings). > Perspective drawing of the bare fuselage framework (skin removed), seen from the front-left and slightly above, nose end at lower left and tail at upper right. Twelve transverse frames ('Couple I' to 'Couple XII') are labelled from front to rear. The front end (frames I-III) is an open-topped cockpit bay with arched frames and diagonal (X) cross-bracing in the floor/sides; a tall built-up structure stands on the top of the framework behind the cockpit; aft of it the frames become closed oval/elliptical rings of decreasing size, tied by longitudinal stringers and heavy longerons (drawn as thick lines along the sides). The framework ends at the sternpost, which continues upward as a tapered fin post with a round fitting and rivet rows. The upper part of the sheet (above the drawing) carries empty ruled title-block lines.

*Notes: No dimensions. Signature bottom right faint ('J. LERE'?). Folio '63' (written vertically) and archive stamp at lower left.*

Above the upper beam rises a tall, narrow, round-topped arch (the headrest/turnover pylon), joined to the beam by large fillets with stepped shoulders; a riveted fitting sits on top of the arch and another at the bottom of the ring. Small notches around the outer edge of the ring take the stringers. Two pulley brackets hang below the lower beam. Right: 'Détail montage de poulie sur couple', an enlarged view of one pulley bracket: a riveted angle plate under the lower beam carrying a control-cable pulley in a fork.

> Shaded perspective drawing (no call-outs) of the sternpost and the members fixed to it, drawn with the post lying horizontally across the sheet. The sternpost is a long flanged plate member, ending at one end (left) in a short projecting pin and at the other (right) in a rounded, deepened end plate. Along the left and middle portion four deep web plates with oval lightening holes and angle stiffeners stand at an angle from it. The right-hand end plate carries a riveted doubler with rows of holes, two large flanged circular bearings, oval lightening holes, angle brackets, two deep channel members and two half-tube (trough-section) members whose flanged round bases are bolted to the plate.

> Perspective 'phantom' view of the whole aircraft in faint outline (nose at left, left wing toward the top of the sheet, right wing toward the bottom, tail at upper right) with the flight-control linkages drawn in heavy line and identified by boxed labels. The control stick and its rocking lever and the rudder-bar assembly sit in the cockpit; push-pull rods run aft along the fuselage, guided by pulleys on frames 6 and 12, to the elevator control horn at the central elevator hinge and to the rudder horn. For the ailerons, a rod runs from the stick's rocking lever to a bellcrank on rib 22 at each wing root, then outboard along the wing through relays between ribs 15-16 and between ribs 10-10 bis to a horn driving the aileron at rib 6.

*Notes: All labels legible. 'Gauchissement' (literally 'warping') = lateral (aileron) control; 'volet de profondeur' = elevator. Folio '69' and archive stamp.*

Two enlarged details on the right: Detail A shows a gear-train box on the canopy sill (one plain shaft and two splined/threaded shafts carrying meshing spur gears, plus a sprocket with a chain) — the drive mechanism of the sliding hood; Detail B shows a pin with a notched head projecting downward from a bracket on the hood frame and entering a slotted block on the sill, where it is retained by a transverse sliding bolt with a slotted mushroom head — the jettison lock; withdrawing the bolt frees the pin so the hood can leave the rails.

> Single shaded perspective drawing of the rudder bar, without callouts. A long beam, tapering from its centre towards both ends and finished at each end with a round disc-shaped end plate, pivots about a vertical axis on a cast pedestal: a lightened triangular bracket (two triangular-web legs with cut-outs) standing on a flat base with a bolt-hole lug at front and rear for attachment to the cockpit floor structure. On top of the beam on each side is a flat foot plate with upturned edges (pedal plate); the near one shows a row of nine holes (two of them larger) along its front edge, for the foot-strap / pedal position adjustment. Between the plates the top of the beam carries two parallel slotted bars with a series of bolts, apparently allowing the pedal plates to be adjusted along the bar.

*Notes: No dimensions or reference numbers on this plate. Draughtsman's signature at bottom: 'LABORIE LE 12.9.38' (last digit read as 8, could be 9 — medium confidence). Handwritten archive folio '71'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES' (inner text illegible). Several pieces of tape visible along top and bottom edges.*

> Phantom perspective of the rear fuselage (tail to the left, nose to the right) with the skin cut away, showing the cable run of the tailplane incidence adjustment. A handwheel ('see Detail A') mounted on the far-side upper longeron at the cockpit drives a cable loop aft, via a pulley bracket at D on a vertical member and a swivelling pulley at C under the upper longeron, to a screw jack at B mounted on the last fuselage frame at the root of the tailplane leading edge. Detail A: three-spoke handwheel whose hub is a two-groove cable drum, with a small locking lever in the hub. Diagram: the upper cable (solid line) and lower cable (dashed line) leave the drum tangentially and are wrapped on it in opposite senses, crossing over the hub. Detail B: vertical screw with a square upper end, running in a nut body clamped by two strap clamps to the frame (frame shown dotted), with the cable drum/pulley at the base of the nut and the cables leaving it horizontally. Detail C: double pulley in a swivelling bracket with a triangular mounting lug. Detail D: double pulley enclosed in a box-shaped bracket with a triangular mounting flange.

R. MOULIS [draughtsman's signature]
D. 520 . S
[handwritten folio] 75
[stamp] ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES (Departmental Archives) of Haute-Garonne

> Cut-away perspective of the inboard part of one wing panel (tip toward upper left, root and fuselage at lower right), with skin panels removed along the rear of the wing to show the flap drive. A long spanwise torque tube runs along the rear spar parallel to the trailing edge; at three stations along the span a crank on the tube drives, through an adjustable link (rod with clevis ends), a flap rib/hinge fitting housed in a box between ribs, each box showing a curved quadrant member and a diagonal strut. At the root the torque tube passes through the root rib ('nervure d'encastrement') and, via a universal joint and a short splined shaft supported in a bearing bracket, enters the fuselage (the 'paroi de la coque' — fuselage shell wall — is shown cut). Inside the fuselage, on the floor structure, a transverse shaft carries a central lever/bellcrank driven by a horizontal actuator (jack) lying fore-and-aft, and continues to the opposite wing. Large circular lightening holes and access openings are visible in the spar web near the root.

*Notes: No dimensions. Signature lower left 'R. MOULIS' (small). Handwritten archive folio '75'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la Hte-Gne'. Draughtsman's initial is blurred on this scan (could read A.); read as R. by comparison with the clear signature 'R. MOULIS' on Pl. 22 (p-098).*

> Shaded perspective of the flap actuator installation in the fuselage. A long cylindrical jack is mounted by trunnions in a split bearing block on a pedestal bracket, which stands on a riveted triangular-web member (lightened by slotted holes) running between frame 3 (couple 3, lower left) and frame 4 (couple 4, right). The jack's piston rod, with a lock-nut, ends in a fork that is pinned to a lever (bellcrank arm) clamped by a bolt onto a transverse tube; this tube is the cross-shaft that drives the flap torque tubes of both wings (Pl. 20). The far end of the jack cylinder has a hexagon fitting and a union for the pressure line.

*Notes: No dimensions. No draughtsman signature visible. Handwritten archive folio '76'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la Hte-Gne'.*

> Main view: cut-away perspective of one flap drive station at the wing trailing edge. At left the rear spar with the cut flap torque tube; a crank on the tube drives, through link A, a bellcrank pivoted on a riveted fitting at the top of the rear spar; the flap station rib carries a curved quadrant (arc) guide and a diagonal strut, and the flap box section tapers to the trailing edge at right; lower link plates with bolts join the mechanism to the flap. Detail A: the link, with fork ends at both ends and a central tube shown partly cut away to reveal an internal threaded rod/coil (adjustable link). Lower-left inset 'Diagram of flap operation': chordwise section of the wing trailing edge (AILE) and flap (VOLET), flap retracted in solid hatching and lowered in dashed outline; arrows show the crank ('guignol') on the torque tube turning anticlockwise, pushing a link aft to rotate a bellcrank on the rear spar, whose lower arm pushes a horizontal link aft, which drives the flap along the curved quadrant guide so that the flap moves aft and swings downward about a pivot located just below the wing lower surface.

*Notes: No dimensions or angles written. Caption abbreviated on the sheet as 'DU VOLET DE COURB^re' (= courbure). Signature 'R. MOULIS' clearly legible here (same draughtsman as Pl. 16 and Pl. 20, whose initial was read there as A.). Handwritten archive folio '77'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la Hte-Gne'.*

> Perspective drawing of the welded-tube engine mount, seen from above, front and one side, with the fireproof bulkhead (firewall frame) indicated by a dotted arched outline at the rear (left). Two long upper engine longerons (bearers, 1 right and 2 left) with bolt-hole flanges for the engine feet run forward and slightly downward; they are braced by diagonal tubes back to the attachment points on the firewall frame and down to a lower triangulated structure whose front nodes (3 lower front left, 4 lower front right — a tubular bushing) carry the lower front of the mount. Labels at left mark the upper attachment ('attache supérieure') near the top of the firewall arch and the lower attachment ('attache inférieure') at its foot; a plate fitting below the far side, labelled 'attache bâti moteur sur voilure', attaches the mount to the wing. Callout 5 marks the upper left fitting at the top of the firewall arch, 6 the lower fitting where a diagonal joins the cross tube near the firewall. A parts table at right lists items 1-6 (Repères / Désignation / Secteur; Secteur column empty).

*Notes: No dimensions. Table entries use abbreviations: 'SUP^r' = supérieur, 'INF^r' = inférieur, 'AV' = avant, 'SUP^re' = supérieure. 'SECTEUR' column empty. Signature lower left read as 'Jean Laffille 3.8.39' (surname spelling medium confidence; date 3 August 1939). Handwritten archive folio '78'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la Hte-Gne'.*

Bottom left: plan view from above, nose left (right side at top): two parallel bearers with drilled engine-foot plates (3 holes + 1 hole on the front plate, 2 on each following plate), an X-braced cross bay with a transverse tube at the lower front nodes (6 right, 3 left), and the rear tubes converging into fork ends for the frame-1 attachments, with central links 7 (right) and 2 (left). *Notes: This sheet (dated 30-5-40) is a later, three-view version of the engine mount than Pl. 23 (p-099, 3.8.39); the item numbering differs between the two plates. > Shaded perspective (no callouts) of the complete rear fuselage cone with the tail unit, seen from ahead and above, with the open forward joint of the cone (showing internal stringers and the stepped skin joint) at left. The fin, faired into the fuselage by a long curved dorsal fillet, is metal-skinned with riveted panels; the rudder hinge line runs down its rear, and above the fin's tip rib the rudder extends forward in a horn balance carrying a small oval fitting. The lower part of the rudder trailing edge carries a long narrow tab with a small operating rod near its lower end. The tailplane halves are mounted low on the tail cone ahead of the rudder, with a prominent rounded root fairing (fillet) at the junction; each half has rounded tips and an elevator whose leading-edge line steps forward near the tip (horn balance at the elevator tip) and shows another step at about mid-span.

*Notes: No dimensions or callouts. Handwritten archive folio '80'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la Hte-Gne'. The small oval on the rudder horn is unexplained on this plate (could be a light or balance fitting).*

A riveted tubular leading-edge spar runs the full height of the rudder (with a joint collar near its lower end); above it the horn balance is a skinned box projecting forward over the spar top, with a cylindrical nose. Six horizontal ribs (flanged, with circular lightening holes) join the spar to the curved trailing-edge member, the top one braced by a diagonal; in the lower aft part a vertical auxiliary spar (from the third rib to the bottom rib) closes a skinned trailing-edge box (tab area), on which a small fitting is visible. > Perspective of the complete one-piece tailplane spar, a long riveted box/I-section beam that is deepest at the centre and tapers in depth toward both tips; two triangular gusset fittings stand up at the centre section, each carrying lugs underneath. Three circular enlargements: upper — the spar tip end with a small angle fitting (two holes) riveted to the web; lower left — one centre fitting from below, showing two parallel lugs (double lug with a bore) bolted to the lower flange through a packing piece ('cale'); lower right — the other centre gusset from the side, with rows of rivets and 'embases Simmonds' (Simmonds anchor-nut bases) on the lower flange beside the twin lugs.

*Notes: No dimensions. Signature lower left 'R. PUECH LE 19/8/39' (initial and day medium confidence). 'Embases Simmonds' = Simmonds anchor-nut bases (period fastener make, kept verbatim). Handwritten archive folio '84'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la Hte-Gne'.*

> Perspective of the two elevator halves laid end to end diagonally across the sheet, joined at the centre by a short splined/threaded coupling between their tubular spars; the tailplane outline is indicated by a long dashed line ahead of them. The upper (far) half is shown as bare framework: a tubular leading-edge spar in sections, about six ribs with triangular gusset webs, a curved trailing-edge member, and a skinned horn-balance nose at its tip. The lower (near) half is shown mostly skinned with two cut-outs revealing ribs, a notch (hinge cut-out) in the leading edge at about mid-span, and a skinned, rounded horn at the tip. Upper right: a plain planform outline of one elevator half (root at upper left, tip at lower right) with two small rectangular hinge cut-outs in the leading edge and two hatched strips labelled 'Compensation statique' — one on the leading edge just inboard of the outer hinge cut-out, the other in the leading edge of the tip horn.

*Notes: No dimensions. Title uses 'volet' for the elevator (also on Pl. 27 'articulation centrale de volet'); translated as elevator per drawing content, NOT the wing flap (volet de courbure). Signature 'R. MOULIS' lower left. Handwritten archive folio '85'. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la Hte-Gne'. Small stray marks at the right edge (fold/tape).*

> Two shaded drawings. Left: perspective of the tail-skid installation in the rear fuselage, seen from below/aft — a pair of curved frame plates with large lightening holes joined to upper and lower longitudinal members (G, J), with the skid leg (F) and its small wheel in a fork projecting below, the movable panel (E) and its attachment (D) around the leg. Right: larger side view of the tail-skid unit alone: a long inclined shock-absorber strut pinned at its top, bearing on a horizontal skid body ('corps de béquille') that pivots at its forward end about the hinge pin; at the rear end a vertical bearing carries the castoring leg (F) with a pulley/collar at the top, and a triangular bracket (D) carries the movable panel (E) that closes the opening around the leg.

*Notes: Glossary rule applied: 'béquille' translated as tail skid although the drawing shows a small wheel (no 'roulette' in text). Letters G–K have no legend on the plate. Handwritten folio '89'; archives stamp; pencil marks/tape at top left and bottom.*

An engine-driven compressor at the front lower left of the engine feeds a pipe running aft under the engine and through the firewall to a control unit with a long hand lever in the cockpit, which also has outlets for gun cocking, tyre inflation, the brakes, a drain, a separator and the bottle-filling connection; two compressed-air reservoirs lie low in the fuselage behind the cockpit. From the control unit a pipe runs forward over the fuel tank and firewall to the distributor at the rear top of the engine, from which pipes go to a row of non-return valves on the cylinders and to injectors on the induction pipes. > Perspective (from the left front, above) of the cooling-water circuit drawn as shaded pipes over a faint outline of the V-12 engine. Two tall header tanks ('nourrices d'eau') with a de-aerator and safety valve stand at the front of the engine; their down-pipes and the cylinder-block outlets join, via Durit hose joints, at the engine-driven pump low at the rear of the engine. From the pump two large pipes run aft to the two end header boxes of the radiator, drawn at lower right as a long rectangular matrix block with vertical cooling louvres; return pipes rise to the cylinder-block inlets at the rear. Arrows give the flow direction. A half-section detail of the safety valve is at upper right.

*Notes: This sheet replaces an earlier Pl. 35 ('annulant la Pl. 35'). Signature at lower left is a handwritten scrawl (illegible). Handwritten folio '92'; archives stamp; stains on the sheet.*

> Left half of the Pl. 37 fold-out: side elevation of the nose and forward fuselage in faint outline with the fuel system in red. Fuel from the right and left wing tanks (the left one via a pipe passing through the spar) reaches a collector low behind the firewall, which also has a tapping on the central tank and a gauge; a hand pump in the cockpit is connected to it. From the collector, pipes pass the firewall to the 5-way fire cock and the engine-driven left and right pumps, then via a non-return valve to a manifold feeding the three left-hand carburettors along the lower side of the engine; a drain carries away fuel condensed in the supercharger piping. The central tank's gauge and pressure-gauge relay are at the top behind the firewall, with an air intake above the decking.

> Schematic plan of the engine fire-detection and extinguishing system. Six hatched rectangles in two rows of three represent the carburettors (three per side), with a detector (small cylinder) beside each; ejector nozzles (circles) at the front corners and near the pump are fed by pipes running along both sides of the engine. At the rear (right) are the hatched pump, the two magnetos (rings) with their detectors and the detection collector, a multi-branch fitting next to the large circular 'Lévy' G4 extinguisher bottle, which feeds the ring of pipes.

*Notes: Handwritten folio '95'; archives stamp.*

> Single-view, shaded pictorial drawing of the whole instrument panel seen from the pilot's seat, with 38 underlined callouts on leader lines arranged left and right. The panel has a broad arched (segmental) top; engine instruments are grouped in a block of six square-flanged dials at lower left with the propeller-pitch box, a lighting/switch box at upper left, a tall central housing carrying the Vion compass over the flight-control airspeed dial and turn indicator, flight instruments (variometer, altimeter, clock) and the flare-control box to the right of centre, and the Gourdou oxygen inhaler and Ericsson cockpit relay at lower right. Below the centre is a horizontal strip of armament switches (cocking buttons and trigger toggles) above a fuse box, and a narrow pedestal extends downward carrying the fuel gauge, the triple pressure gauge and the brake-pressure gauge.

*Notes: Callout-to-object mapping traced on enlarged crops; uncertain ones: FEUX DE ROUTE (leader ends at the box's upper-left corner — assigned to the left toggle), ANEMO (leader passes behind the left knob — assigned to second toggle), SIGNALISATION DU TRAIN (arrow touches the small knob beside the 4-lamp box), INDICATEUR DE VIRAGE (leader runs down the housing edge — assigned to the bottom window). Clock and altimeter have no callouts. Small plate texts on the HÉLICE box, gear box and B.E.C. box only partly legible. Draughtsman signature bottom left partly illegible ('R. M[oulis?]'). Handwritten archive folio '96' and Archives départementales stamp bottom right.*

> Wiring diagram laid out as a plan: a vertical trunk of multi-wire conduits runs down the fuselage centre from the engine (generator, Ratier regulator, relay and distribution boxes, starting coil) at the top to the cockpit equipment (control panel AF 1631 R, shielded boxes, fuse box, compass, reflector sight, thermometers, gear indicator) and on to the tail lights at the bottom; a horizontal trunk crosses it to each wing. Each wing branch carries the wingtip navigation light (left red, right green), undercarriage contacts, a flare and a flare launcher, the left wing also the pitot head and landing light. Conduit diameters and wire counts/gauges are written beside each run.

*Notes: Much of the small italic handwriting is faint; bracketed items are uncertain or illegible. Wire gauges are in mm² (as written: 0,63 / 1,25 / 2,5 / 5). Handwritten folio '98'; archives stamp.*

> Perspective cut-away view of the machine-gun ammunition box installed in the wing between the main spar (Longeron, behind/above) and the false spar (Faux-longeron, front/below in the view), crossing the rib tails (Queues de nervure). The lid (Couvercle) is shown lifted off above the box; it carries two pairs of ammunition indicators (Indicateurs de munitions) and a handle lock. The box is cut open to show the folded ammunition belt; at one end a feed chute (Couloir d'alimentation pour mitrailleuses) leads the belt out to the machine gun, held by locking bolts. The box pivots about a hinge axis along its lower edge (Axe de pivotement de la boîte). A circular detail enlarges the hinge/attachment fitting of the chute.

*Notes: Lettering is clear. 'Blocage de la poigné' written without the final 'e' on the plate. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES' (Haute-Garonne) and handwritten folio number '105' at lower right. Box is drawn in perspective; spanwise orientation inferred.*

> Schematic of the electro-pneumatic armament control system. Along the top are drawn, from left to right: two left-wing machine guns with their compressed-air bottle and two solenoid valves (D and A), the engine-mounted cannon in the centre with its cocking cylinder, valves A and D and its own air bottle, the gun camera (cine-mitrailleuse), and two right-wing machine guns with their air bottle and valves A and D. Pneumatic lines (double lines) and electrical wires (single and dashed lines) run down to two valve blocks marked D / 30 / A (one per wing, labelled MI G and MI D, with 'sortie du fuselage' break marks where the lines leave the fuselage) and a central A / D block for the cannon. In the centre is the cockpit switch panel: lever switches (security switch, warning lamp), push-buttons grouped under ARMEMENT (MG, C, MD) and DÉTENTE (MG, C, MD), a row of six fuses, and the trigger contacts D and DR on the control stick. A cockpit air bottle with pressure gauge and charging connection appears at the bottom.

*Notes: Note: on this copy, Pl. N° 50 is titled 'Schéma électro pneumatique' (not 'Positionnement des armes'). Meanings of A = armement (cocking) and D = détente (firing) are inferred from the ARMEMENT / DÉTENTE panel headings; 'DR', '30' and '33' are not explained on the plate. Draughtsman's name at lower left faint, read as 'DEFFES E.' (low confidence). Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES de la H[au]te-G[aron]ne' and a partial handwritten folio number at lower right (cut off at page edge).*

> Fold-out plate (Pl. 50 bis, operating manual) in three views showing where the weapons sit. Top left: a front view of the aircraft with the propeller hub (engine cannon on the centreline) and two gun muzzles side by side in each wing, just outboard of the main undercarriage legs. Top right: a partial side view of the forward fuselage with the cannon (red) along the engine axis and vertical dimensions between the line of sight (LIGNE DE VISÉE), the cannon axis (AXE DU CANON) and the mean axis of the wing machine guns (AXE MOYEN DES MITRAILLEUSES). Bottom left: a plan view (nose pointing down the sheet) showing the two pairs of wing machine guns (each line labelled MITRAILLEUSE) with their spanwise distances from the cannon axis, the aircraft axis (AXE APPAREIL), the line of sight, the cannon axis and a note that the engine mount is set (offset) at 1°. Drawn by A. Labraulet, February 1940.

*Notes: Plate is a faint pencil-like red drawing; all dimension figures are small hand lettering near the resolution limit of the scan. Readings of 1743 and 41 are fairly clear; 1840 (second digit 8 vs 6), 168 (last digit 8 vs 2) and 160 (first digit underlined) are medium; the side-view vertical figures 505 (alt. 565/306) and 626 (alt. 686) are written sideways and checked against the enlarged half-sheet scans p-146/p-147 (same plate, 'Planche N° 50 bis Détails'). A small figure near 'AXE APPAREIL' is partly illegible (probably '1°', possibly '10'). Units not stated; assumed millimetres. Note that in this manual Pl. 50 (Notice descriptive) is the electro-pneumatic diagram, and this sheet is Pl. 50 bis of the Notice d'utilisation. Signed 'A. LABRAULET  Février 1940' at lower left. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES ... H[au]te-G[aron]ne' and handwritten folio '107' at lower right.
Audit fix: gun-position figures re-read on the enlarged detail scan p-147 with glyph comparison: 1743 (third digit is the same open italic 4 as in 1840), 1840 (second digit has a small closed top loop = 8, unlike the long-ascender 6 of 168), 168 (last digit has a closed top loop = 8, not 2), 160 (middle digit has the long ascender of a 6, not 8). All four readings retained.*
