> Perspective view from the front left of the engine cowling and the left wing root, with the cowling cut away to show the exhaust stubs of the left cylinder bank. Cold air enters at the front of a tube running along the exhaust manifold (a heat exchanger), and the heated air is led through a flexible 'boa' hose and clamps, then an insulated tube through the wing leading edge into the machine-gun compartment, which is shown opened (in red) with the guns and ammunition-box compartment. The leading-edge gun openings are sealed with glued fabric patches (toile obturatrice collée) and part of the area is covered with Émaillite-doped fabric. The note states that the right wing is symmetrical.

*Notes: Plate number written 'PL. N° 50 ter' (superscript 'ter'). A faint draughtsman's name and date at the bottom centre are illegible. Small marks on the exhaust stubs are illegible. Archive stamp 'ARCHIVES DÉPARTEMENTALES' and handwritten folio '108' at lower right.*

> Perspective installation drawing, seen from below, of the electrical (and associated pneumatic) control runs for the wing guns. A cable bundle runs from the fuselage side (right of sheet, 'to engine mount', 'electrical line coming from the right side') along the wing spar/rib flanges, held by numbered clamps at dimensioned intervals, to the machine-gun bay where the cocking and firing solenoid valves (électro-valves armement / détente) are mounted with flexible pipes, and to an S.G.E. junction box with two sockets. The ammunition-box compartment with its large lightening holes is shown in the middle. An inset 'Vue suivant flèche' (view in direction of arrow) at upper left shows the firing solenoid valve on the gun-support rib. Notes call for notching the gun-support rib for the valve wire, a 4.5 × 4.5 chamfer for a nut, and sleeving of all wiring; the air supply comes from the 8-litre bottle.

*Notes: Plate number written 'PL. N° 50⁴' (i.e. 50 quater). Many small handwritten figures: 29[5] (last digit uncertain) and [9]0 (could be 40) on the upper run are low-confidence; a small vertical dimension near the S.G.E. box is illegible; the label read '[2] MAP' near the gun bay is uncertain; last word of the chamfer note is illegible (b[oulon?]). Large pink stain/blot at lower left obscures part of the drawing. Archive stamp and handwritten folio '109' at lower right.*

> Three pictorial (photo-derived) illustrations of moving the aircraft on the ground, without captions. Left: the aircraft with its tail resting on a two-wheeled tail trolley, towed by a man with a long tow bar while two mechanics push on the wing leading edge/root. Inset circle (lower right): the tail trolley under the rear fuselage towed by a farm-type tractor. Upper right: the aircraft towed forward from the main undercarriage by a V-shaped towing bridle (cables from each main leg to a central tow bar) held by a man in flying kit who signals with his arm, while another man walks at the wingtip. Arrows link the inset to the two main scenes.

> Pictorial three-quarter front view of the aircraft lifted clear of the ground by two hoists, each with a short spreader bar. The forward spreader carries two vertical cables, one on each side of the engine cowling, hooked to a transverse lifting bar/fitting under the front fuselage just aft of the exhaust stubs and above the wing leading-edge root. The rear spreader carries two cables to a transverse bar through the rear fuselage just ahead of the tailplane and above the tail wheel. A mechanic in a cap stands at the nose directing the operation. The undercarriage is down; roundels are visible on the wing upper surfaces near the tips and 'D 520' is painted on the fin.

R. Badin [?] [draughtsman]

> Three perspective views of one rudder pedal assembly: a pedal tread (ribbed foot plate with heel rest and toe flange, ending in a circular foot plate) mounted on a sliding block that runs along a pair of parallel channel/bulb-section rails bolted together. The pedal can be pinned in a series of holes along the rails to adjust reach. The two smaller views at left show the extreme (fully forward and fully aft) positions, the large view at right the mean position, with the adjustment bolts and holes visible.

*Notes: No dimensions. Faint draughtsman's name at lower left, read as 'R. Badin' (low confidence). Archive stamp and handwritten folio '118' at lower right; a small dark paper fragment/tab is visible near the bottom centre.*

> Partial side view of the fuselage (nose to the left, cut off at the cowling and ahead of the tail) showing a small circular access door in the fuselage side just behind the cockpit, below the rear part of the canopy, set in a riveted reinforcing doubler and hinged at its top edge. Enlarged details show: the door hinge arm in the closed position (top left); a section through the door with its pressed stiffening sheet (A), rubber sealing cord (B) and spot welds (C) (top centre); a section through the fuselage skin around the opening (centre right); and the suitcase rack (porte-valise) inside, a frame with cross-members spanning between frames 7 and 8 and holding a case with crossed bungee cords (top right). A key box at lower right explains A, B, C.

*Notes: Signature 'R. VINCENT 18.7.39' with a partly illegible number in brackets '(18[?])' at lower left. Archive stamp and handwritten folio '119' at lower right.*

> Plate in three views locating every inspection door. Top left: front view (fuselage door 7 indicated on the aircraft's left side). Bottom left: plan view from above, nose towards the bottom of the sheet, showing the wing doors (single-circle numbers 1-12, repeated on both wings; doors drawn solid are on the upper surface, dotted on the lower surface) and the fuselage doors (double-circle numbers). Right: side profile (nose to the right) with fuselage doors 1-10 (dotted = left side, solid = right side). A two-column legend table lists the wing (VOILURE) and fuselage (FUSELAGE) doors.

*Notes: Legend text clear. In wing items 2 and 5 the text is given as ditto marks (») under 'Renvoi de commande d'aileron'; fuselage items 2, 3, 7 use ditto marks for 'Accès'; fuselage item 4 reads only 'AU RÉSERVOIR D'ESSENCE' (implied 'Accès'). Whether door V12 is solid or dotted is hard to tell (appears dotted). NB: Pl. 57 (p-161) draws the baggage door on a profile with the nose to the left, which would suggest the right side; this plate's note and front view place it on the LEFT side. Signed 'R. LARROQUE' at lower left. Archive stamp and handwritten folio '121' at lower right.
Audit fix: fuselage door 10 'boîte (de) récupération' retranslated as the cannon spent-case collecting box (was 'oil/vapour catch box'); printed p.38 ('Vidange des étuis') places the cannon's case-collecting box behind an inspection door on the LEFT side cowling, matching door 10 and the left-side gas vent pipe on this plate.*

> Perspective view from the front of the aircraft with one wing raised on a tripod lifting jack (vérin de relevage) placed under the wing outboard of the main undercarriage; the jack head engages a fitting reached through an inspection door (porte de visite) in the wing lower surface, circled. At right, enlarged details of the fitting on the rear face of the wing main spar: up to aircraft No. 200, a riveted box bracket with a hinged tie-down ring shown stowed and the jack's cup-shaped head engaging the bracket from below; from No. 201, a riveted bracket with a bolted U-stirrup (tie-down ring) and a socket underneath for the jack.

*Notes: Lettering clear. Faint pencil handwriting along the jack base at bottom left is illegible. A pasted paper repair/stain near the right wingtip. Archive stamp and handwritten folio '123' at lower right.*
